# Who is she? Reader 03

Reader 03 of 3; baby-development; read 2026-11-17. Working evidence for the final merging agent, not a finished avatar or findings page.

All 935 assigned records were read in full: quote, context, speaker, stage, themes and source. This share is entirely Reddit material, originally selected for question 2 (pain), not a representative survey, purchaser sample or app-review sample. Counts below are counts of quote records, never people or market prevalence. Several excerpts and crossposts can describe one experience. Counts overlap across patterns and must not be added into a headcount.

| File | ID prefix | Records |
|---|---|---:|
| `quotes/reddit-ur02.jsonl` | `dv22` | 184 |
| `quotes/reddit-ur03.jsonl` | `dv23` | 241 |
| `quotes/reddit-ur04.jsonl` | `dv24` | 248 |
| `quotes/reddit-ur05.jsonl` | `dv25` | 262 |

All paths here are relative to `baby-development/` unless stated. Each ID resolves to its original quote record, URL and stored source. Only complete stored quote fields are reproduced as blockquotes: no new quotations from raw posts, trimming, cleaned spelling, changed euphemisms or inferred diagnoses. Raw-source information is explicitly marked as a paraphrased context check below.

The primary thematic counts use self-accounts by identified mothers or parents whose gender is unspecified. Unspecified parents are not assumed to be mothers. Clearly paternal/non-maternal voices, external/relayed accounts, the fictitious story and the no-self-context records are counted separately. An identified mother can still be observing someone else's baby; that observation is not turned into the other mother's autobiography. Retrospective material is labelled. Categories are broad retrieval patterns for the merge: a rule-related quote does not necessarily endorse the same rule as another quote in that category.

The source speaker fields contain 256 new_mother, 55 experienced_mother, 1 recovered_parent, 569 parent_unspecified, 39 partner, 10 relative_caregiver, 3 professional and 2 advisor records. These are original labels, not a fresh gender or parity estimate; the explicit relaying/fiction/context checks override them where necessary. Source stages: 32 newborn, 68 baby 2–4 months, 47 baby 4–6 months, 78 baby 6–9 months, 157 baby 9–12 months, 267 toddler 12–18 months, 162 toddler 18–24 months, 45 looking_back and 79 unknown. These describe the child/context, not the adult's age.

## What is unknown in the excerpts

This audit uses the stored quote plus its recorded context only. It deliberately does not copy information discovered in one opened original into every excerpt from that post. A missing excerpt-level cue is not proof that the original post never supplies it. Raw checks below add examples, not a complete demographic census.

| Explicit cue in quote/context | Quote records with it | Without that cue |
|---|---:|---:|
| Work, study, stay-home status, return to work or household employment | 45 | 890 |
| Explicit first-time/first-child identification | 37 | 898 |
| Adult chronological age stated in the stored quote itself | 0 | 935 |
| A positive named location or named health system | 4 | 931 |
| Numerical personal/household earnings in the stored quote itself | 0 | 935 |

The first-child absence is not a parity-unknown count: many other records name siblings or another pregnancy. Income is not inferred from toys, insurance, childcare, a job or a currency. Work cues include partners' employment and one relayed work decision; they do not all say the mother herself is employed.

Complete work-cue IDs: dv22-0019, dv22-0025, dv22-0031, dv22-0032, dv22-0036, dv22-0047, dv22-0070, dv22-0073, dv23-0025, dv23-0108, dv23-0126, dv23-0145, dv23-0147, dv23-0150, dv23-0191, dv23-0203, dv23-0241, dv24-0046, dv24-0069, dv24-0097, dv24-0119, dv24-0146, dv24-0168, dv24-0178, dv24-0188, dv24-0202, dv25-0047, dv25-0048, dv25-0057, dv25-0124, dv25-0148, dv25-0165, dv25-0171, dv25-0187, dv25-0188, dv25-0192, dv25-0195, dv25-0197, dv25-0217, dv25-0218, dv25-0223, dv25-0224, dv25-0239, dv25-0258, dv25-0259.

Complete first-time-cue IDs: dv22-0009, dv22-0031, dv22-0046, dv22-0181, dv23-0058, dv23-0141, dv23-0166, dv23-0194, dv23-0200, dv24-0037, dv24-0038, dv24-0047, dv24-0057, dv24-0058, dv24-0063, dv24-0067, dv24-0080, dv24-0083, dv24-0084, dv24-0096, dv24-0102, dv24-0115, dv24-0131, dv24-0133, dv24-0140, dv24-0161, dv24-0167, dv24-0171, dv24-0202, dv24-0215, dv24-0219, dv24-0243, dv24-0248, dv25-0089, dv25-0108, dv25-0129, dv25-0132.

Named place/system IDs: dv22-0053, dv22-0142, dv23-0036, dv23-0094. The NHS is a health-system clue; South Florida is a place; the two UK statements are explicit. dv25-0205 explicitly excludes being US-based but does not name the country; dv22-0133, dv25-0246 discuss an unnamed country. A UK subreddit by itself is not counted as residence.

Raw context supplies stated maternal ages including 20, 24, 26, 28, 35 and 38, and age 40 at childbirth; it does not establish a typical buyer age. See dv23-0199, dv23-0241, dv24-0090, dv24-0126, dv24-0131, dv24-0161, dv24-0202, dv25-0042, dv25-0045, dv25-0070, dv25-0246. Ages of a partner, relative, older sibling or child are not hers. In dv24-0022 the narrator is 20 and the mother/cousin is 24, second-hand; in dv23-0044, dv24-0051, dv24-0148 ages of wives are supplied by another parent. Education is also only used where stated: Early Childhood Education/development, Education Studies, psychology study, online college/GPA and pre-K teaching appear in opened sources or excerpts, not an inferred education bracket (dv23-0008, dv23-0025, dv23-0203, dv24-0065, dv24-0131, dv25-0045).

Raw location examples include England, Texas, central California, Alabama, north of Atlanta, NY and North Carolina (dv23-0016, dv23-0099, dv23-0159, dv23-0210, dv24-0126, dv24-0148, dv24-0161, dv25-0190, dv25-0242). Norway is a home-language clue in dv23-0233, not a country-of-residence finding. A father living in Mexico or a sister-in-law in France does not locate the mother (dv24-0065, dv24-0126).

## Her situation

### First-time parents

First-time parenthood is explicitly stated in some records; it is not a property of the whole share.

Count: 35 supporting quote records.

The first-time count uses explicit wording in the stored quote or context, rather than the new_mother speaker label. Record dv24-0123 describes limited infant experience without saying first child and is discussed as novice experience below, not counted as first-time.

Strong or distinctive supporting quotes:

[dv23-0058]
> I’m a first time mom and so it’s hard to know what’s normal. Logically I know babies go at their own pace but I also don’t want to be neglectful.

[dv23-0200]
> His extremely spoiled being our first and only child, has hundreds of toys, including,  kitchens,  open- ended toys, ride on cars, tonies boxes etc etc.

[dv24-0058]
> I’m a FTM and clearly her boys are surviving, so she probably wouldn’t like any advice from me. But looking in from the outside, it’s worrisome.

[dv24-0080]
> I’ve been listening to the podcast Montessori way and have been interested by following this lifestyle but on a budget. It’s my first child and so far I try my best to implicate some of the self-regulation, independent play, screen free, independent feeding but I want to help facilitate more for the educational aspect like body parts, colours and such. Letters were getting good at as he had a mind explosion recently is way more vocal and willing to talk- I have an alphabet book and letter cards that we use for that.

[dv24-0063]
> He’s mostly happy and energetic and meets all his milestones early, he’s reached every 2 year old milestone on the CDC chart except for combining 2 words. I feel just exhausted by him and am wondering why he won’t eat or sleep or why he’s so rough and poorly behaved often. Just feeling defeated as a first time mom.

### Other children and another pregnancy

Older siblings, twins, stepchildren and another pregnancy make attention and play a family scheduling problem.

Count: 116 supporting quote records.

This category includes planning another child as well as already having siblings. Raw context adds a disabled older child, bereavement, wide age gaps and closely spaced pregnancies; those details are not inferred from birth order words alone.

Strong or distinctive supporting quotes:

[dv22-0011]
> He loves tummy time and will often fall into a deep, restful sleep while on his stomach (always under close supervision), but of course I know stomach sleeping isn't safe for unsupervised sleep.

[dv22-0035]
> Sitting here right now, I can actually say I’m happy and enjoying my baby! (I didn’t enjoy my first born until he was like 5 months old!) I don’t dread the days and I don’t even get the evening scaries anymore.

[dv22-0039]
> She was a preemie born 6 weeks early and 4lbs. She has hypotonia and is gross motor delayed. She didn’t walk until 20 months and never crawled-i.e. we had to hold/carry her for a majority of her life.

### Work, staying home and study

Paid work, staying home and study all appear, and care must fit around those commitments.

Count: 36 supporting quote records.

The work count uses a cue about the author or household's work/study status in the stored quote/context. Raw checks additionally identify five months maternity leave, return at eight weeks, twelve-hour shifts, self-employment, leaving jobs, a breadwinner, and a military deployment. These do not establish a typical occupation or income.

### Practical help and its limits

The availability, reliability and emotional cost of help shape what the parent can do.

Count: 161 supporting quote records.

A partner living in the house is not counted as proof of shared care. Temporary solo care, single motherhood and widowhood are distinct; the opened originals identify each. Having a nanny or a supportive spouse does not necessarily remove guilt or the feeling of being on call.

Strong or distinctive supporting quotes:

[dv22-0019]
> And my other problem is that motherhood is already extremely hard even with a “healthy” child, in quotation marks. If she now also has some kind of impairment, I have no idea how I would cope with that, especially because my partner is not particularly supportive. 
> 
> He has his job, but childcare mostly falls to me. 
> 
> That means everything would stay hanging on me. 
> 
> All of it  all the examinations, everything would stay hanging on me.

[dv22-0025]

### Costs, constraints and expensive choices

Money enters through medical access, childcare, equipment and gifts, with very different room to spend.

Prices are the speaker's reports. Insurance claims are not out-of-pocket spending; willingness to pay and contemplated purchases are not purchases. ### Medical histories and the parent's own health

Infant illness, prematurity, disability, birth trauma and the parent's own health can organize family life.

Count: 285 supporting quote records.

These are self-reports or reports of clinicians' statements, not diagnoses by this reader. A medically complex-child subgroup must not become the assumed biography of every buyer. The originals also show parents whose babies are healthy or easy while the parent still lacks guidance or is distressed.

### The home, transport, pets and weather

Physical space, pets, transport, weather and relocation restrict which play and self-care options are practical.

Count: 122 supporting quote records.

Raw context distinguishes a rented townhome, apartments, shared multigenerational housing, a ranch, a cabin and temporary lodging. Country is not inferred from a subreddit, units, spelling or a fear of shootings. A car seat, carrier or stroller can itself prevent outings.

## Her day

### Care and supervision fill the gaps

Feeding, settling, cleaning, contact and supervision compete with shared play and ordinary personal tasks.

Count: 555 supporting quote records.

A few hours of prescribed stimulation cannot be read as free time. The counts include accounts of workable routines and enjoyable play as well as days in which basic care consumes capacity. Repeated accounts and multiple excerpts from one routine remain separate quote records.

### Sleep and tiredness structure time

Night waking, short or contact naps and changing sleep needs determine when the parent can work, rest or do chores.

Count: 169 supporting quote records.

The raw of record dv24-0115 explicitly places writing in bed beside a contact-napping baby; record dv24-0131 describes overnight rumination and writing on two hours sleep; record dv24-0046 describes posting after bedtime. These examples do not establish a usual search or posting hour. Archive timestamps are not used as local time.

### Adult contact and personal time

Care can restrict adult conversation, outings, bodily autonomy and time to feel like a separate person.

Count: 160 supporting quote records.

Isolation can persist during frequent outings and in the presence of nearby support. The originals distinguish a deliberate stay-home choice from illness, work, abuse, transport problems or another adult being away. The fictional isolation passage is retained separately.

### Searching, posting and recording

Parents turn everyday uncertainty into searches, questions, comparisons, tracking and requests for other families' experience.

Count: 160 supporting quote records.

Searching is not automatically a source of reassurance. A parent may want concrete outcomes or relevant ages/conditions rather than more instructions; another says tracking worsens the experience. Time of searching is usually unstated.

## Who else has a say

### The other parent

Partners influence the division of care and disagree over screens, sleep, discipline, medical help and what counts as enough effort.

Count: 102 supporting quote records.

Preserve both praise and conflict from the same account. The primary story can describe the father as hands-on while rejecting his proposed CIO, or describe a spouse doing chores while she handles nearly all childcare. Accounts of rough handling, violence or coercion are attributed testimony, not softened into a generic disagreement.

Professional reassurance, criticism, conflicting assessments, referrals and waits change how parents see themselves and their babies.

### Friends, relatives' babies and other parents

Real-life peers supply comparisons, advice and companionship, and can make ordinary parenting feel judged.

Count: 141 supporting quote records.

A sibling or friend can be supportive rather than competitive; several parents deliberately step back from competition. An older child with disability or a long sibling age gap can be a poor reference point for the new baby.

### Daycare, nurseries, nannies and sitters

Childcare is judged through the baby's behaviour, developmental feedback, trust, cost and fit with family schedules.

Count: 64 supporting quote records.

A happy daycare report can conflict with distress at home; a developmental report can conflict with daily good-day messages. Sending a child can be sought for socialization, language or respite, not only employment. Plans, attendance and being refused continued care are distinct.

### Rules for a good parent

Parents test themselves against rules about safety, stimulation, independence, attachment, screens, feeding and discipline.

Count: 353 supporting quote records.

The complete support list is broad because the rules differ. It does not count all these records as believing any single rule. Some connect screens to delay, some to learning; some want early teaching, others explicitly reject rushing development. Statements about causation or neurobiology remain the author's belief.

### Limits of control and fitting the actual child

Some parents explicitly accept variation, reject rushing or reject advice that does not fit their child.

Count: 82 supporting quote records.

Acceptance can coexist with uncertainty or wanting intervention. Avoid turning a parent who understands developmental variation into someone who never worries, or intervention-seeking into a wish for an exceptional child.

Strong or distinctive supporting quotes:

## What she has already tried or bought

### Hands-on attempts

Parents have already tried play, narration, reading, movement practice, routines, outings, redirection and other ways to manage the day.

Count: 425 supporting quote records.

Attempting an activity is not evidence of effectiveness. Some results improve, some are brief, some fail and some are harmful actions the parent discloses. Selection is detailed below rather than treating the parent as someone who has tried nothing.

### Equipment, toys, books and screens

Existing equipment and media are used, rejected, adapted or supplemented, while other items are still being considered.

Count: 339 supporting quote records.

Having many toys is not proof of high income, and asking for toy ideas is not a product purchase. Useful distinctions here include failed carriers/strollers, rejected infant gear, household-object play, gifts cluttering a small room, adaptation of a playpen into a fort, and screens that either do or do not buy a break.

## How she sees herself

### Uncertainty and monitoring

Uncertainty can keep the parent checking, seeking reassurance and anticipating harm even when others say the baby is doing well.

Count: 407 supporting quote records.

This is a broad uncertainty/monitoring category, not a diagnosis of anxiety and not the count believing autism is present. Severe self-reported anxiety and treatment are distinguished in the source notes; some posts are simply practical questions.

Strong or distinctive supporting quotes:

[dv22-0063]
> im not in full blown convinced “he’s autistic” mode, he’s only 8 months. but god do i have theee deep feeling that may only be a fear, but it consumes me everyday as i interact with my son (which is constant)…. im constantly engaging, modeling & teaching, but constantly looking for red flags/signs.

[dv23-0086]
> I should add he’s recently gotten much more confident with his walking so often takes off, and inevitably tumbles. 99% of the time it’s fine. Maybe once a week he has a fall that scares him (even if he’s not injured) eg flat backwards or onto his face.

[dv24-0113]

[dv24-0206]

### Failure, blame and professional judgment

Parents describe feeling inadequate, guilty, ashamed or responsible for their child's difficulties or for needing a break.

Count: 162 supporting quote records.

The standard can be contradictory: leaving a content child alone feels like neglect, using a playpen feels like cheating, failing to teach feels neglectful, while forcing practice feels cruel. Fear of CPS judgment at a home visit is not an admission of neglect. Explicit harmful behaviour is separately retained, not conflated with these fears.

Strong or distinctive supporting quotes:

[dv22-0028]
> I’m struggling with mom guilt because if they are a-word, there’s nothing wrong with that, but I keep feeling like I’m failing them.

[dv22-0099]
> After seeing everything first hand, I vowed to never have children if that was a possibility…. Clearly I went back on that vow and it is hard to feel as though I am not being punished for it.

[dv23-0027]
> Like why did I only play pretend with her? Why didn’t I persistently read to her? Why did I let life get in the way of me educating my daughter? I am so embarrassed. Beyond embarrassed. I hated myself before but holy I actually am reaching a new low.

[dv23-0107]
> I’ve been having scary intrusive thoughts because of all of this, I would never act upon them and I feel shameful having dark thoughts. I love my kids more than anything and I just wish I could do more for them.

[dv25-0055]
> Hi guys, I have a 16 m.o. boy whom we allowed formula bottles for too long. We always kept delaying it and I regret it now.

[dv25-0083]
> This has escalated to the point where I feel anxious whenever she comes near me because the biting really hurts. Nowadays, I lose my temper almost instantly due to this anxiety, and as a result, I raise my voice. Today, I actually had to leave the room just to ensure I wouldn't accidentally hurt her while pushing her away.

[dv25-0165]
> When it was just me and my son at home some days we watched a lot of tv.

[dv25-0221]

### Whose version of motherhood she compares with

Comparisons with other babies, siblings, partners and online parents can change the parent's judgment of their own competence.

Count: 116 supporting quote records.

An online milestone, a cousin's word count or a quieter sibling is not a population norm. One parent cautions that milestone memories themselves require a diary/video check; another rejects competition explicitly.

Strong or distinctive supporting quotes:

[dv23-0202]
> The constant judgement I get from doing what is psychologically best for my son is really getting me down.

[dv24-0182]
> My older child was a very “clingy” baby but he outgrew it as he got older, my daughter is the opposite, she was way easier to please as a small baby and seems to rely on me more and more as she grows.

[dv25-0111]
> It’s making me feel like a bad mom though :( like if I’m not constantly with him he gets distressed or that I ruin his play time when he’s happy. I’m honestly so exhausted even if I’m not showing it outwardly.

### Pride, enjoyment and improving days

Parents also describe pleasure, connection, successful adaptations and pride in supporting the actual child.

Count: 107 supporting quote records.

Pride can coexist with exhaustion, grief or guilt. Perceived benefit from a program, video, routine, medication or surgery remains attributed to the parent, not established causal effectiveness.

Strong or distinctive supporting quotes:

[dv22-0004]
> We have been doing everything in our power to get this girl to put weight on her arms, but she is miserable. She just wants to stand. She gets so happy and clearly proud of herself when she pulls herself up!

[dv23-0132]
> She plays very well with her 9 month old brother. She’s gentle and giggles a lot with him. She helps me with everything.

[dv23-0205]
> My son loves stacking blocks, we learned! He likes making a high stack and trying to make the blocks straight. He has big blocks at home but apparently likes small ones more. I'll try to get him some.

[dv24-0131]
> We love our baby boy so much and he’s in a fun stage right now of squealing and being very curious about everything. He’s typically a very sweet and happy baby.

[dv24-0143]

[dv24-0204]
> Now... He had his wake window - he even ROLLED for the first time (back to belly to back to belly!!!).

[dv24-0243]

[dv25-0181]

### The emotional and physical cost

Parents report boredom, depletion, anger, grief, physical pain or loss of enjoyment as part of this care.

Count: 449 supporting quote records.

Boredom is not treated as lack of love, and saying the baby is loved is not treated as evidence that the day is tolerable. Sensitive accounts below retain suicidal thoughts, ambivalence, relationship abuse, loss, disability fears, intrusive thoughts and actual harmful actions, with their provenance.

Strong or distinctive supporting quotes:

[dv24-0027]
> I just want to help her, but ultimately I am already grieving a future I thought we WOULD have if she would have been NT

[dv24-0120]
> The missed and late milestones aren’t even what is causing me so much grief! He physically abuses me for every waking moment of the day. He bites, scratches, pinches, slaps, and now he’s got these new orthotic boots he wears that he smashes my fingers with. His PT believes these will help to encourage him to walk so I deal with them with hope in my heart but my gosh it’s just one more way for him to hurt me now.

[dv24-0151]
> I HATE when people offer to “take the baby off of my hands” because now I’m not doing anything because Im stressed being away from my baby, but I’m also stressed in general.

[dv25-0122]
> If not, any suggestions on how to keep this kid entertained in an almost 6 hour wake window is highly appreciated 🫠

[dv25-0213]

Parents describe the identity they want and the person they feel they have become, including conflict with interests, independence and their own childhood.

Count: 285 supporting quote records.

Self-description includes perfectionist, activity/weekly-theme mom, exhausted primary parent, parent trying not to repeat abuse, a person missing adult stimulation, and a mother wanting a reciprocal bond. The raw checks supply stated ages, education, employment and health details without importing new quotations.

## Experiences that need their own labels

### Severe distress, abuse, intrusive thoughts and harmful actions

Severe distress, abuse, unwanted thoughts, decisions about another pregnancy and disclosures of actual harm occur and must be distinguished.

Count: 51 supporting quote records.

These are not interchangeable. Record dv22-0045 says she feels rage and hate but knows she does not hate her baby. Records dv23-0040, dv24-0218, dv25-0152, dv25-0221, dv25-0224 disclose self-harm or suicidal thoughts; record dv25-0218 is the father's crisis. Record dv23-0107 describes unwanted thoughts without intent. Records dv24-0009, dv25-0004, dv25-0005 describe the father's unwanted bodily response and intrusive thoughts. Records dv22-0100, dv22-0101 report witnessing partner roughness/suspected ongoing harm; 188, 460, 583 and 586–587 disclose actions, not merely thoughts. Record dv25-0234 describes forcing a bottle into the child's mouth. Record dv25-0036 raises an unconfirmed abuse suspicion; do not convert it to an abuse finding. Records dv23-0041, dv25-0045, dv25-0089 have pregnancy termination/retrospective abortion considerations in the opened originals. Sources dv24-0090, dv25-0045 report assault-related conception and abusive maternal homes. Disability fears and the doctor's quoted slur in record dv22-0064 remain intact, with attribution. Record dv24-0244 describes recovery from postpartum psychosis/addiction in the original. None of this is removed because it would be unsuitable for copy.

## Who is speaking who is not her

### Fathers and other non-maternal parents

Fathers and other non-maternal parents describe both similar guilt and different care, work, relationship or bodily experiences.

Count: 50 supporting quote records.

The source partner label covers 39 records; reading identifies additional paternal voices and two partner reports about a mother. Some are expectant or looking back. Records dv24-0009, dv25-0004, dv25-0005 are versions of an unwanted-erection/intrusive-thought account, not distinct parents and not clinical advice. Records dv24-0075, dv24-0076 are the partner's claims about the mother. The father giving up food and working 80+ hours, and the stay-at-home father with gaming guilt, are not mothers' self-reports.

## Repetition and provenance for the merge

Exact repeated stored quote fields form 11 groups. Keep the record counts, but do not treat them as independent testimony:

- dv22-0149, dv22-0178.
- dv23-0051, dv25-0046.
- dv23-0060, dv25-0059.
- dv23-0061, dv25-0060.
- dv23-0080, dv24-0078, dv25-0088.
- dv23-0090, dv25-0102.
- dv23-0182, dv24-0192.
- dv23-0216, dv24-0225.
- dv24-0011, dv25-0007.
- dv24-0136, dv25-0144.
- dv25-0120, dv25-0123.

Near-duplicates and crossposts also remain: the difficult five-month-old accounts across dv22-0007, dv22-0008, dv22-0013, dv22-0014, dv22-0025; the developmental-delay account across dv22-0064, dv22-0065, dv22-0066, dv22-0067, dv22-0068, dv22-0098, dv22-0099; swaddling/leap repeats in dv22-0011, dv22-0012, dv22-0128, dv22-0129, dv22-0149, dv22-0155, dv22-0178; the daycare choices in dv23-0060, dv23-0061, dv25-0059, dv25-0060; pregnancy/toddler-tower accounts in dv23-0080, dv23-0081, dv23-0082, dv24-0078, dv24-0079, dv25-0088, dv25-0089; speech home-visit/CPS concerns in dv23-0182, dv23-0183, dv24-0192, dv24-0193; grandmother preference in dv23-0129, dv23-0130, dv24-0133, dv24-0134; the father's intrusive-thought versions in dv24-0009, dv25-0004, dv25-0005; and the repeated well-involved-father dispute in dv23-0090, dv25-0102, dv25-0103. Possible promotional intent is labelled for dv23-0208 because the original moves into a named dressing recommendation; it is not asserted as proven advertising. The sociologist's history in dv24-0025 is an attributed argument, not collected survey evidence. The English wording of a partner's insult in dv24-0076 is the poster's own translation. The fictional passage dv25-0179 is retained in full above and excluded from real-life claims. No AI authorship is inferred merely from prose style. Medical, legal, benefit and developmental-causation claims remain attributed accounts; no network verification was performed or requested.

## Coverage and verification

Unread assigned records: 0. All four files and all 935 IDs are accounted for. No source was excluded because of sensitivity, and no source files were changed. The no-self-context ledger explains the child-only excerpts; external, retrospective, promotional-risk and fictional material have separate labels.

Programmatic check: every blockquote is compared with the entire stored quote field for its ID, preserving punctuation, wording, case, whitespace and Unicode; every ID mentioned anywhere in this file must resolve to this share. Pattern counts are checked against the complete supporting-ID sets. The report contains no quotations copied from raw-only text.

Coverage: 935 quotes in the share; 754 distinct quotes reproduced; 181 further IDs listed without reproduction; 36 quotes said nothing additional about who the adult is under the excerpt-level definition above; 0 unread; 935 IDs accounted for.
